\displaystyle 2\left[p^{2}\xi_{100}^{\left(-1\right)}+\left(p\cdot q\right)\xi_{010}^{\left(-1\right)}+\left(p\cdot r\right)\xi_{001}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p^{2}\xi_{000}^{\left(-1\right)}-\left[\xi_{00}^{\left(0\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}+\left[\xi_{00}^{\left(0\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime}

(3.190)

Second relation

\displaystyle 2\left[\left(p\cdot q\right)\xi_{300}^{\left(-2\right)}+q^{2}\xi_{210}^{\left(-2\right)}+\left(r\cdot q\right)\xi_{201}^{\left(-2\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{2}\xi_{200}^{\left(-2\right)}+\left[\xi_{\text{{two}}}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}-\left[\xi_{20}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime}

(3.191)

\displaystyle 2\left[\left(p\cdot q\right)\xi_{210}^{\left(-2\right)}+q^{2}\xi_{120}^{\left(-2\right)}+\left(r\cdot q\right)\xi_{111}^{\left(-2\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{2}\xi_{110}^{\left(-2\right)}-\xi_{100}^{\left(-1\right)}+\left[\xi_{\text{{one}}}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}

(3.192)

\displaystyle 2\left[\left(p\cdot q\right)\xi_{201}^{\left(-2\right)}+q^{2}\xi_{111}^{\left(-2\right)}+\left(r\cdot q\right)\xi_{102}^{\left(-2\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{2}\xi_{101}^{\left(-2\right)}+\left[\xi_{\text{{one}}}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}-\left[\xi_{11}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime}

(3.193)

\displaystyle 2\left[\left(p\cdot q\right)\xi_{120}^{\left(-2\right)}+q^{2}\xi_{030}^{\left(-2\right)}+\left(r\cdot q\right)\xi_{021}^{\left(-2\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{2}\xi_{020}^{\left(-2\right)}-2\xi_{010}^{\left(-1\right)}+\left[\xi_{20}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}

(3.194)

\displaystyle 2\left[\left(p\cdot q\right)\xi_{111}^{\left(-2\right)}+q^{2}\xi_{021}^{\left(-2\right)}+\left(r\cdot q\right)\xi_{012}^{\left(-2\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{2}\xi_{011}^{\left(-2\right)}-\xi_{001}^{\left(-1\right)}+\left[\xi_{11}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}

(3.195)

\displaystyle 2\left[\left(p\cdot q\right)\xi_{102}^{\left(-2\right)}+q^{2}\xi_{012}^{\left(-2\right)}+\left(r\cdot q\right)\xi_{003}^{\left(-2\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{2}\xi_{002}^{\left(-2\right)}+\left[\xi_{02}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}-\left[\xi_{02}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime}

(3.196)

\displaystyle 2\left[\left(p\cdot q\right)\xi_{100}^{\left(-1\right)}+q^{2}\xi_{010}^{\left(-1\right)}+\left(r\cdot q\right)\xi_{001}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{2}\xi_{000}^{\left(-1\right)}-\left[\xi_{00}^{\left(0\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}+\left[\xi_{00}^{\left(0\right)}\right]^{\prime}

(3.197)

Third relation

\displaystyle 2\left[\left(p\cdot r\right)\xi_{300}^{\left(-2\right)}+\left(q\cdot r\right)\xi_{210}^{\left(-2\right)}+r^{2}\xi_{201}^{\left(-2\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{2}\xi_{200}^{\left(-2\right)}+\left[\xi_{\text{{two}}}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}-\left[\xi_{20}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]

(3.198)

\displaystyle 2\left[\left(p\cdot r\right)\xi_{210}^{\left(-2\right)}+\left(q\cdot r\right)\xi_{120}^{\left(-2\right)}+r^{2}\xi_{111}^{\left(-2\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{2}\xi_{110}^{\left(-2\right)}+\left[\xi_{\text{{one}}}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}-\left[\xi_{11}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]

(3.199)

\displaystyle 2\left[\left(p\cdot r\right)\xi_{201}^{\left(-2\right)}+\left(q\cdot r\right)\xi_{111}^{\left(-2\right)}+r^{2}\xi_{102}^{\left(-2\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{2}\xi_{101}^{\left(-2\right)}-\xi_{100}^{\left(-1\right)}+\left[\xi_{\text{{one}}}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}

(3.200)

\displaystyle 2\left[\left(p\cdot r\right)\xi_{120}^{\left(-2\right)}+\left(q\cdot r\right)\xi_{030}^{\left(-2\right)}+r^{2}\xi_{021}^{\left(-2\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{2}\xi_{020}^{\left(-2\right)}+\left[\xi_{20}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}-\left[\xi_{02}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]

(3.201)

\displaystyle 2\left[\left(p\cdot r\right)\xi_{111}^{\left(-2\right)}+\left(q\cdot r\right)\xi_{021}^{\left(-2\right)}+r^{2}\xi_{012}^{\left(-2\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{2}\xi_{011}^{\left(-2\right)}-\xi_{010}^{\left(-1\right)}+\left[\xi_{11}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}

(3.202)

\displaystyle 2\left[\left(p\cdot r\right)\xi_{102}^{\left(-2\right)}+\left(q\cdot r\right)\xi_{012}^{\left(-2\right)}+r^{2}\xi_{003}^{\left(-2\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{2}\xi_{002}^{\left(-2\right)}-2\xi_{001}^{\left(-1\right)}+\left[\xi_{02}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}

(3.203)

\displaystyle 2\left[\left(p\cdot r\right)\xi_{100}^{\left(-1\right)}+\left(q\cdot r\right)\xi_{010}^{\left(-1\right)}+r^{2}\xi_{001}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{2}\xi_{000}^{\left(-1\right)}-\left[\xi_{00}^{\left(0\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}+\left[\xi_{00}^{\left(0\right)}\right]

(3.204)

Analogously to the
\xi_{00}^{\left(0\right)}
, whose analysis was
developed in Equation (
3.160
), expressing
\xi_{abc}^{-1}
-like
functions in terms of
\xi_{abc}^{-2}
-like functions is convenient. To
accomplish this task, employ
Q^{-1}=Q^{-2}Q
as a link between both
families. Following the procedure from the referred case and using
\xi_{\text{{one}}}^{\left(-1\right)}=\xi_{00}^{\left(-1\right)}-\xi_{10}^{\left(-1\right)}-\xi_{01}^{\left(-1\right)}
, we obtain the
relations that concern this investigation:

\displaystyle\xi_{000}^{\left(-1\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m^{2}\xi_{000}^{\left(-2\right)}-\left[p^{2}\xi_{100}^{\left(-2\right)}+q^{2}\xi_{010}^{\left(-2\right)}+r^{2}\xi_{001}^{\left(-2\right)}\right]+\left[\xi_{00}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime},

(3.205)

\displaystyle 2\xi_{100}^{\left(-1\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m^{2}\xi_{100}^{\left(-2\right)}-\left[p^{2}\xi_{200}^{\left(-2\right)}+q^{2}\xi_{110}^{\left(-2\right)}+r^{2}\xi_{101}^{\left(-2\right)}\right]+\left[\xi_{\text{{one}}}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime},

(3.206)

\displaystyle 2\xi_{010}^{\left(-1\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m^{2}\xi_{010}^{\left(-2\right)}-\left[p^{2}\xi_{110}^{\left(-2\right)}+q^{2}\xi_{020}^{\left(-2\right)}+r^{2}\xi_{011}^{\left(-2\right)}\right]+\left[\xi_{10}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime},

(3.207)

\displaystyle 2\xi_{001}^{\left(-1\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m^{2}\xi_{001}^{\left(-2\right)}-\left[p^{2}\xi_{101}^{\left(-2\right)}+q^{2}\xi_{011}^{\left(-2\right)}+r^{2}\xi_{002}^{\left(-2\right)}\right]+\left[\xi_{01}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}.

(3.208)

3.4
Explicit Perturbative Amplitudes

After understanding the structure of correlators at the
integrand level, we developed a strategy to deal with divergences associated
with their integration. The objective of this section is to perform this
operation explicitly. For each case, the first step is evaluating Feynman
integrals since these are the fundamental pieces that build up the
investigated objects. Subsequently, we obtain standard tensors and
perturbative amplitudes hitherto identified.

3.4.1
Two-Point Amplitudes - Feynman Integrals and
AV

Our task is to compute quantities introduced in Subsection (
3.2.2
), with a particular interest in the
AV
correlator. That is also the
opportunity to elucidate elements related to the strategy. After detailing
the procedure for the separation, we organize ill-defined mathematical
quantities through standard divergent objects. Posteriorly, we evaluate
finite contributions using common tools of perturbative calculations, such
as Feynman parametrizations and finite loop integration. One might consult
further information about these resources in introductory books on quantum
field theories
[
49
]
.

We achieved the
AV
structure in Equation (
3.48
) through a contraction
with the standard tensor (
3.40
). Considering the antisymmetric
character of the Levi-Civita symbol, the simplified integrand arises

t_{\mu\nu}^{AV}=4i\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\left[k_{1}^{\alpha}k_{2}^{\beta}\frac{1}{D_{12}}+\left(k_{1}-k_{2}\right)^{\alpha}\frac{k^{\beta}}{D_{12}}\right].

(3.209)

Denoted by an uppercase letter, the amplitude combines the following
two-propagator Feynman integrals (
3.120
):

T_{\mu\nu}^{AV}=4i\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\left[k_{1}^{\alpha}k_{2}^{\beta}I_{2}+\left(k_{1}-k_{2}\right)^{\alpha}I_{2}^{\beta}\right].

(3.210)

Since this expression exhibits a divergent power counting, we adopt a
prescription to propagator-like objects
D_{n}
through identity (
3.129
).
The separation is successful if the identity considers
N
as equal to or
higher than the power counting of the integral. Thus,
N=2
would be a
logical option as two-point amplitudes have quadratic power counting in the
physical dimension. Nevertheless, we acknowledged simplifications due to the
antisymmetric character of the
AV
, which allows using the
N=1
version.
Although both routes lead to the same outcome, the first generates more
finite contributions and involves more laborious calculations.

Alternatively, one might also evaluate Feynman integrals separately,
adopting versions for the identity as it finds suitable. We opt for this
route because these integrals also emerge within higher-order amplitudes.
For instance, as the
I_{2}
integral exhibits logarithmic power counting
when integrated, employing the
N=0
identity version rewrites the
propagator-like structure
D_{1}
and splits its integrand as follows

\frac{1}{D_{12}}=\left[\frac{1}{D_{\lambda}}-\frac{A_{1}}{D_{\lambda}D_{1}}\right]\frac{1}{D_{2}},

(3.211)

where denominators involve
D_{n}=\left(k+k_{n}\right)^{2}-m^{2}
and
D_{\lambda}=k^{2}-\lambda^{2}
and numerators exhibit the object
A_{n}=2k_{n}\cdot k+k_{n}^{2}+\lambda^{2}-m^{2}
.

Power counting decreased as required, so the last contribution will generate
a finite integral. Nevertheless, the first term still shows diverging power
counting (when integrated). Exploring both propagator-like objects is
necessary for this term, so divergent objects depend only on non-physical
quantities, i.e., the loop momentum
k
and the scale
\lambda^{2}
. Such a
property is intrinsic to the IReg. Therefore, by employing the identity for
D_{2}
within this specific term, the separation assumes the form

\frac{1}{D_{12}}-\frac{1}{D_{\lambda}^{2}}=-\frac{A_{2}}{D_{\lambda}^{2}D_{2}}-\frac{A_{1}}{D_{\lambda}D_{12}}.

(3.212)

This organization puts ill-defined mathematical structures on the left-hand
side of equations after integration, so it is transparent that the
right-hand side leads to a finite quantity. Therefore, by identifying the
irreducible divergent object (
3.134
), we have the
I_{2}
integral:

I_{2}\left(k_{1},k_{2}\right)-I_{\log}=-\int\frac{d^{4}k}{\left(2\pi\right)^{4}}\left[\frac{A_{2}}{D_{\lambda}^{2}D_{2}}+\frac{A_{1}}{D_{\lambda}D_{12}}\right].

(3.213)

Our next task is to compute the finite part; however, dealing with products
in the denominators is inconvenient. One generally rewrites these structures
through Feynman parametrizations to avoid such circumstances. This resource
expresses rational functions in terms of an integral representation; observe
the examples:

\frac{1}{ab}=\int_{0}^{1}dz\frac{1}{\left[\left(b-a\right)z+a\right]^{2}},

(3.214)

\frac{1}{abc}=2\int_{0}^{1}dz\int_{0}^{1-z}dy\frac{1}{\left[\left(b-a\right)y+\left(c-a\right)z+a\right]^{3}},

(3.215)

\frac{1}{abcd}=6\int_{0}^{1}dz\int_{0}^{1-z}dy\int_{0}^{1-y-z}dx\frac{1}{\left[\left(b-a\right)x+\left(c-a\right)y+\left(d-a\right)z+a\right]^{4}},

(3.216)

where
x
,
y
, and
z
are parameters. Variations employed here emerge
through derivatives with respect to
a
, which increases its power on the
left-hand side.

Let us clarify this subject by adopting a variation of (
3.214
) to
express the first finite contribution from the scalar integral (
3.213
).
After replacing
a=D_{\lambda}
and
b=D_{2}
, we group terms on the loop
momentum by completing the square

-\int\frac{d^{4}k}{\left(2\pi\right)^{4}}\frac{A_{2}}{D_{\lambda}^{2}D_{2}}=-2\int_{0}^{1}dz\left(1-z\right)\int\frac{d^{4}k}{\left(2\pi\right)^{4}}\frac{A_{2}}{\left[\left(k+k_{2}z\right)^{2}+P_{1}\right]^{3}};

(3.217)

therefore, one polynomial dependent on the arbitrary routing arises

P_{1}\left(z\right)=k_{2}^{2}z\left(1-z\right)+\left(\lambda^{2}-m^{2}\right)z-\lambda^{2}\text{.}

(3.218)

Performing a shift on the variable
k+k_{2}z\rightarrow k
makes the
denominator momentum-even while generating an additional term in the
numerator, which allows identifying a derivative of the polynomial:

-\int\frac{d^{4}k}{\left(2\pi\right)^{4}}\frac{A_{2}}{D_{\lambda}^{2}D_{2}}=-2\int_{0}^{1}dz\left(1-z\right)\int\frac{d^{4}k}{\left(2\pi\right)^{4}}\left[2k_{2}^{\rho}k_{\rho}+\frac{\partial P_{1}}{\partial z}\right]\frac{1}{\left(k^{2}+P_{1}\right)^{3}}.

(3.219)

Any finite integral found in one-loop calculations leads to this type of
structure after parametrization. Nevertheless, derivatives (and their
powers) only appear if the original integral has divergent power counting.
The next step consists of the loop integration, which only produces non-zero
contributions for even integrands; the case above yields:

\int\frac{d^{4}k}{\left(2\pi\right)^{4}}\frac{1}{\left(k^{2}+P_{z}\right)^{3}}=\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\frac{1}{2P_{1}}.

(3.220)

Posteriorly to replacing this result, one must integrate by parts until all
derivatives are eliminated. This case requires a sole operation and leads to
the outcome:

\displaystyle-\int\frac{d^{4}k}{\left(2\pi\right)^{4}}\frac{A_{2}}{D_{\lambda}^{2}D_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dz\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\left(1-z\right)\ln P_{1}+\ln P_{1}\right]

(3.221)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dz\ln\frac{P_{1}}{-\lambda^{2}}.

Finite contributions follow a strong pattern since we departed from a
logarithmically divergent integral. Each step described above has an
analogous form in the second contribution from Equation (
3.213
). The
fundamental difference is in the parametrization (
3.216
), which
involves two propagators and leads to another polynomial

\displaystyle P_{2}\left(z,y\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle k_{1}^{2}y\left(1-y\right)+k_{2}^{2}z\left(1-z\right)-2k_{1}\cdot k_{2}yz

(3.222)

\displaystyle+\left(\lambda^{2}-m^{2}\right)y+\left(\lambda^{2}-m^{2}\right)z-\lambda^{2}.

Observe how this dependence reflects on the integration by parts:

\displaystyle-\int\frac{d^{4}k}{\left(2\pi\right)^{4}}\frac{A_{1}}{D_{\lambda}D_{12}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dz\int_{0}^{1-z}dy\frac{\partial}{\partial y}\ln P_{2}

(3.223)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dz\ln\frac{Q}{P_{1}}.

The lower limit of integration (in
y=0
) returns the first polynomial;
hence, this type of term disappears when summing up the entire sector. Even
in more complex cases, finite contributions involving arbitrary routings
k_{i}
cancel out identically in a chain effect. Only the term achieved by
applying the upper limit of integration (in
y=1-z
) contributes in the end.
That leads to the dependence on external momentum
p=k_{1}-k_{2}

acknowledged in Subsection (
3.3.2
), embodied in the polynomial:

Q\left(z\right)=p^{2}z\left(1-z\right)-m^{2}.

(3.224)

With both contributions at our disposal, building up the scalar Feynman
integral (
3.213
) is possible

I_{2}\left(k_{1},k_{2}\right)-I_{\log}=-\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\xi_{0}^{\left(0\right)}\left(p\right),

(3.225)

where the finite function was identified (
3.137
). Such an expression
clarifies that the parameter
\lambda^{2}
plays the role of a scale
connecting finite and ill-defined quantities. That becomes transparent by
setting routings as zero
k_{i}=0
on the equation above:

I_{\log}\left(m^{2}\right)-I_{\log}\left(\lambda^{2}\right)=-\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\ln\frac{m^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}.

(3.226)

This type of scale relation is implicit whenever logarithmic functions are
present in this investigation.

After detailing the first case, we directly cast one possible separation
linked to the vector integral
I_{2}^{\beta}
(
3.120
). Since its power
counting indicates linear divergence, let us set
N\leq 1
in identity (
3.129
) and employ both versions to achieve the structure

\left[\frac{k^{\beta}}{D_{12}}\right]_{not\text{ }odd}+2\left(k_{1}+k_{2}\right)_{\rho}\frac{k^{\beta}k^{\rho}}{D_{\lambda}^{3}}=\frac{A_{2}\left(A_{1}+A_{2}\right)k^{\beta}}{D_{\lambda}^{3}D_{2}}+\frac{A_{1}^{2}k^{\beta}}{D_{\lambda}^{2}D_{12}}.

(3.227)

We disregard momentum-odd terms since they vanish with the loop integration.
Again, the adopted arrangement puts ill-defined structures on the left-hand
side:

I_{2}^{\beta}\left(k_{1},k_{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(k_{1}+k_{2}\right)_{\rho}\left(\Delta^{\beta\rho}+g^{\beta\rho}I_{\log}\right)=\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\frac{1}{2}\left(k_{1}+k_{2}\right)^{\beta}\xi_{0}^{\left(0\right)}\left(p\right).

(3.228)

We employed the irreducible object (
3.134
) and the 2nd-order surface
term (
3.133
) to organize the divergent sector. Finite contributions
lead to the family (
3.137
); we also employed the reduction of finite
functions
\xi_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\xi_{0}
, achieved initially in (
3.143
).

Lastly, let us employ the achieved integrals to build the
AV
amplitude (
3.210
). Finite contributions and irreducible divergent objects cancel
out identically after using the identity
\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\left(k_{1}-k_{2}\right)^{\alpha}\left(k_{1}+k_{2}\right)^{\beta}=2\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}k_{1}^{\alpha}k_{2}^{\beta}
.
Hence, the only non-trivial contribution is the following

T_{\mu\nu}^{AV}=-2i\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}p^{\alpha}\left(k_{1}+k_{2}\right)_{\rho}\Delta^{\beta\rho}.

(3.229)

That agrees with the expectation from Equation (
3.49
), i.e., it is a
surface term proportional to an arbitrary momenta combination.

Observing this expression isolated, one might expect that restricting
arbitrary labels (as in
k_{2}=-k_{1}
) would eliminate surface terms and
solve issues approached while exploring symmetry aspects. Nevertheless, that
is not enough when considering the complete discussion. For this reason, we
maintain the arbitrariness associated with labels, so the analysis falls
over values accessible to surface terms.

3.4.2
Three-Point Amplitudes - Feynman Integrals

Our next objective is to compute quantities typical of
calculations involving three-point correlators, starting with the
corresponding Feynman integrals (
3.121
). Afterward, we evaluate standard
tensors and subamplitudes necessary to build the main targets:
PVV
and
AVV
. Since some ingredients also appear when exploring four-point
structures, we broaden their discussion.

As the first couple of integrals is finite, dependence on external momenta
appears from the beginning. In other words, when employing the Feynman
parametrization (
3.215
) and grouping terms on the loop momentum,
denominators exhibit polynomial (
3.147
):

\frac{\left[1,k_{\mu}\right]}{D_{123}}=2\int_{0}^{1}dz\int_{0}^{1-z}dy\frac{\left[1,k_{\mu}\right]}{\left[\left(k+k_{1}-py-qz\right)^{2}+Q\left(y,z\right)\right]^{3}}.

(3.230)

Then, integrating both sides of the scalar version of this equation yields
the first integral. No compensation term appears by shifting the momentum
k+k_{1}-py-qz\rightarrow k
; hence, obtaining this result is straightforward

I_{3}=\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dz\int_{0}^{1-z}dy\text{
}\frac{1}{Q\left(y,z\right)}.

(3.231)

Although this reasoning extends to the vector version, the momentum shift
brings parameter powers to its numerator

I_{3\mu}=-\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dz\int_{0}^{1-z}dy\text{ }\frac{\left(k_{1}-py-qz\right)_{\mu}}{Q\left(y,z\right)}.

(3.232)

Lastly, the 2nd-order tensor is the only integral exhibiting logarithmically
diverging power counting here. We split its integrand by employing the
N=0

identity version (
3.129
) whenever necessary

\frac{k_{\mu\nu}}{D_{123}}-\frac{k_{\mu\nu}}{D_{\lambda}^{3}}=-\frac{A_{3}k_{\mu\nu}}{D_{\lambda}^{3}D_{3}}-\frac{A_{2}k_{\mu\nu}}{D_{\lambda}^{2}D_{23}}-\frac{A_{1}k_{\mu\nu}}{D_{\lambda}D_{123}}.

(3.233)

Terms associated with ill-defined contributions are on the left-hand side,
so we use standard objects introduced in Equations (
3.133
)-(
3.134
) to express them without additional manipulations.

Regarding finite contributions, each rational function requires a different
Feynman parametrization. Although they lead to structures similar to those
above, polynomials depend on non-physical parameters this time. Furthermore,
momentum shifts induce derivatives of these polynomials in the numerators,
requiring integrations by parts. When completing this procedure, most
contributions fit perfectly, and only those depending on external momenta
remain:

\displaystyle I_{3\mu\nu}-\tfrac{1}{4}\left(\Delta_{\mu\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}I_{\log}\right)

(3.234)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dz\int_{0}^{1-z}dy\text{ }\left(k_{1}-py-qz\right)_{\mu}\left(k_{1}-py-qz\right)_{\nu}\frac{1}{Q\left(y,z\right)}

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}\int_{0}^{1}dz\int_{0}^{1-z}dy\text{ }\ln\frac{Q\left(y,z\right)}{-\lambda^{2}}.

The final step for evaluating these Feynman integrals is to project finite
contributions in terms of structure functions from the families (
3.145
)-(
3.146
). Having two parameters highlights some patterns, which
clarifies that these functions do not appear randomly but in tensors having
well-defined properties. We mentioned them in Subsection (
3.3.3
).
Then, following the identifications, we group terms depending exclusively on
external momenta into what we call
J
-tensors. Other contributions
correspond to lower-order Feynman integrals having combinations of the
routing
k_{1}
as coefficients. Such reasoning materializes in the
following organization

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{3},

(3.235)

\displaystyle I_{3\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{3\mu}-\left[k_{1\mu}I_{3}\right],

(3.236)

\displaystyle I_{3\mu\nu}-\tfrac{1}{4}\left(\Delta_{\mu\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}I_{\log}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{3\mu\nu}-\left[k_{1\mu}I_{3\nu}+k_{1\nu}I_{3\mu}\right]-\left[k_{1\mu}k_{1\nu}I_{3}\right],

(3.237)

where
J
-tensors are introduced

J_{3}=\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\xi_{00}^{\left(-1\right)},

(3.238)

J_{3\mu}=\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\left[p_{\mu}\xi_{10}^{\left(-1\right)}+q_{\mu}\xi_{01}^{\left(-1\right)}\right],

(3.239)

J_{3\mu\nu}=\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\left[p_{\mu}p_{\nu}\xi_{20}^{\left(-1\right)}+q_{\mu}q_{\nu}\xi_{02}^{\left(-1\right)}+\left(p_{\mu}q_{\nu}+q_{\mu}p_{\nu}\right)\xi_{11}^{\left(-1\right)}-\tfrac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}\xi_{00}^{\left(0\right)}\right].

(3.240)

Using these tensors to express mathematical structures appearing in
perturbative calculations is already very useful. They are introduced in
reference
[
47
]
as part of the systematization from IReg,
where they allow a compact presentation of the quadruple-vector box
amplitude.

Although that is part of their purpose here, we stress their remarkable
value regarding algebraic manipulations and interpretation of results. Since

J
-tensors concentrate all contributions on external momenta, they are
enough to describe the finite part of physical amplitudes. We consider this
systematization to propose a new perspective, where
J
-tensors are the
fundamental pieces in this analysis. When computing momenta contractions,
for instance, the discussion resorts to their properties as a generalization
of reductions from Section (
3.3
). Without further delay, let us
employ these ideas in the study of three-point functions subsequently.

3.4.3
Three-Point Amplitudes -
PVV

Before computing the
PVV
amplitude, our first task is integrating the
standard tensor having two momenta in the numerator. By integrating Equation
(
3.53
), we expand products and identify the following combination of
Feynman integrals:

\displaystyle T_{3\mu\nu}^{\left(s\right)}\left(k_{i},k_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1+s\right)I_{3\mu\nu}+\left(k_{j}+sk_{i}\right)_{\nu}I_{3\mu}

(3.241)

\displaystyle+\left(k_{i}+sk_{j}\right)_{\mu}I_{3\nu}+\left(k_{i\mu}k_{j\nu}+sk_{i\nu}k_{j\mu}\right)I_{3}.

We adopt general structures, admitting choices for signs and routings. This
expression applies to any denominator
D_{klm}
typical of three-point
calculations and extends other cases (e.g., box) by changing the numerical
subindex. The same pattern manifests in finite tensors, expressing all
finite quantities below. We delimit our focus to the
D_{123}
case for now.

Since there is an intrinsic idea of hierarchy, we start by replacing the
highest-order integral
I_{3\mu\nu}
(
3.237
). Divergent objects and
the 2nd-order
J
-tensor are ready; however, this operation brings new
contributions through lower-order structures. With this, external momenta
p_{i}=k_{1}-k_{i}
appear as multiplicative coefficients of the next
integral
I_{3\mu}
(
3.236
). Its substitution gives continuity to a
chain effect, and now the last integral
I_{2}
(
3.235
) has this type of
coefficient. Once this procedure is over, we achieve the general form

\displaystyle T_{3\mu\nu}^{\left(s\right)}\left(k_{i},k_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\left(1+s\right)\left(\Delta_{\mu\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}I_{\log}+4J_{3\mu\nu}\right)

(3.242)

\displaystyle-\left(p_{j}+sp_{i}\right)_{\nu}J_{3\mu}-\left(p_{i}+sp_{j}\right)_{\mu}J_{3\nu}+\left(p_{i\mu}p_{j\nu}+sp_{i\nu}p_{j\mu}\right)J_{3}.

This procedure is generic, so we resort to it when examining all standard
tensors. We recall that the object
p_{i}
produces three possibilities
here:
p_{1}=0
,
p_{2}=k_{1}-k_{2}=p
, and
p_{3}=k_{1}-k_{3}=q
.

We aim to build the
PVV
(
3.54
) using this tool, so let us reintroduce
its expression by using uppercase letters to characterize the integrated
amplitude

T_{\mu\nu}^{PVV}=-2im\varepsilon_{\mu\nu XY}\left[T_{3XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{2},k_{3}\right)+T_{3XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{3},k_{1}\right)+T_{3XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{2}\right)\right].

(3.243)

The minus sign reflects in their antisymmetry property, hence, canceling the
first row of the general form (
3.242
). Then, setting the different
momenta arrangements, we cast the required versions:

T_{3\mu\nu}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{2}\right)=-p_{\nu}J_{3\mu}+p_{\mu}J_{3\nu},

(3.244)

T_{3\mu\nu}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{3},k_{1}\right)=q_{\nu}J_{3\mu}-q_{\mu}J_{3\nu},

(3.245)

T_{3\mu\nu}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{2},k_{3}\right)=\left(p-q\right)_{\nu}J_{3\mu}-\left(p-q\right)_{\mu}J_{3\nu}+\left(p_{\mu}q_{\nu}-p_{\nu}q_{\mu}\right)J_{3}.

(3.246)

It is straightforward to sum them to find that these objects collapse into
the finite function

T_{\mu\nu}^{PVV}=-4im\varepsilon_{\mu\nu XY}p^{X}q^{Y}J_{3},

(3.247)

which agrees with the expectation from Equation (
3.55
).

3.4.4
Three-Point Amplitudes -
AVV

Our next target is the
AVV
triangle (
3.62
), which contains a tensor
sector besides the vector subamplitude
VPP
. Given the procedure introduced
in the previous case, let us begin this discussion by writing the integrated
form of the 3rd-order standard tensor (
3.61
) through Feynman integrals

\displaystyle T_{3\mu;\nu\alpha}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{l};k_{i},k_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(k_{j}-k_{i}\right)_{\alpha}I_{3\mu\nu}+\left(k_{i}-k_{j}\right)_{\nu}I_{3\mu\alpha}

(3.248)

\displaystyle+\left(k_{j\alpha}k_{i\nu}-k_{i\alpha}k_{j\nu}\right)I_{3\mu}+\left(k_{j}-k_{i}\right)_{\alpha}k_{l\mu}I_{3\nu}

\displaystyle+\left(k_{i}-k_{j}\right)_{\nu}k_{l\mu}I_{3\alpha}+\left(k_{j\alpha}k_{i\nu}-k_{i\alpha}k_{j\nu}\right)k_{l\mu}I_{3}.

We restricted this equation to the minus sign because only antisymmetric
tensors appear throughout this investigation. That also comprehends the
four-propagator version, achieved by changing numerical subindices.

Replacements start with the highest-order integral and follow a hierarchy
until getting to the lowest. Ultimately, finite contributions depend
exclusively on external momenta
p_{i}=k_{1}-k_{i}
since terms associated
with
k_{1}
combinations vanish identically:

\displaystyle T_{3\mu;\nu\alpha}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{l};k_{i},k_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{4}\left[\left(p_{j}-p_{i}\right)_{\alpha}\Delta_{\mu\nu}+\left(p_{i}-p_{j}\right)_{\nu}\Delta_{\mu\alpha}\right]

(3.249)

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{4}\left[\left(p_{j}-p_{i}\right)_{\alpha}g_{\mu\nu}+\left(p_{i}-p_{j}\right)_{\nu}g_{\mu\alpha}\right]I_{\log}

\displaystyle-\left(p_{j}-p_{i}\right)_{\alpha}J_{3\mu\nu}-\left(p_{i}-p_{j}\right)_{\nu}J_{3\mu\alpha}+\left(p_{i}-p_{j}\right)_{\nu}p_{l\mu}J_{3\alpha}

\displaystyle+\left(p_{j}-p_{i}\right)_{\alpha}p_{l\mu}J_{3\nu}+\left(p_{j\alpha}p_{i\nu}-p_{i\alpha}p_{j\nu}\right)J_{3\mu}

\displaystyle-\left(p_{j\alpha}p_{i\nu}-p_{i\alpha}p_{j\nu}\right)p_{l\mu}J_{3}.

That becomes transparent as a consequence of
J
-tensors structures. Even
though we introduced the scalar
J_{3}
for generality, its coefficient
vanishes here due to the unavoidable presence of
p_{1}=0
. As three
routings are available, three non-equivalent configurations of this tensor
are obtainable:

\displaystyle T_{3\mu;\nu\alpha}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{1};k_{2},k_{3}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{4}\left[\left(q-p\right)_{\alpha}\Delta_{\mu\nu}+\left(p-q\right)_{\nu}\Delta_{\mu\alpha}\right]

(3.250)

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{4}\left[\left(q-p\right)_{\alpha}g_{\mu\nu}+\left(p-q\right)_{\nu}g_{\mu\alpha}\right]I_{\log}

\displaystyle-\left(q-p\right)_{\alpha}J_{3\mu\nu}-\left(p-q\right)_{\nu}J_{3\mu\alpha}+\left(q_{\alpha}p_{\nu}-p_{\alpha}q_{\nu}\right)J_{3\mu},

\displaystyle T_{3\mu;\nu\alpha}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{2};k_{3},k_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\left(q_{\alpha}\Delta_{\mu\nu}-q_{\nu}\Delta_{\mu\alpha}\right)+\frac{1}{4}\left(q_{\alpha}g_{\mu\nu}-q_{\nu}g_{\mu\alpha}\right)I_{\log}

(3.251)

\displaystyle+q_{\alpha}J_{3\mu\nu}-q_{\nu}J_{3\mu\alpha}+q_{\nu}p_{\mu}J_{3\alpha}-q_{\alpha}p_{\mu}J_{3\nu},

\displaystyle T_{3\mu;\nu\alpha}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{3};k_{1},k_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\left(p_{\nu}\Delta_{\mu\alpha}-p_{\alpha}\Delta_{\mu\nu}\right)+\frac{1}{4}\left(p_{\nu}g_{\mu\alpha}-p_{\alpha}g_{\mu\nu}\right)I_{\log}

(3.252)

\displaystyle-p_{\alpha}J_{3\mu\nu}+p_{\nu}J_{3\mu\alpha}-p_{\nu}q_{\mu}J_{3\alpha}+p_{\alpha}q_{\mu}J_{3\nu}.

When looking into integrands, we made expectations regarding these
structures (
3.65
)-(
3.67
). The main point is the impossibility of
building a 3rd-order tensor with the property of total antisymmetry in this
particular context. Having all ingredients required for the verifications,
we comment on them in the sequence.

First, all terms vanish by contracting the Levi-Civita symbol with the first
configuration above since they correspond to products between symmetric and
antisymmetric objects. Whereas most cases are straightforward, inspecting
the
J
-vector content (
3.239
) is necessary for completing this
verification

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\nu XYZ}T_{3X;YZ}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{1};k_{2},k_{3}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon^{\nu XYZ}p_{Y}q_{Z}J_{3X}

(3.253)

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\nu XYZ}p_{Y}q_{Z}\left[p_{X}\xi_{10}^{\left(-1\right)}+q_{X}\xi_{01}^{\left(-1\right)}\right]=0.

Second, all terms cancel out identically when summing these three
configurations. Again, that requires a closer look inside the
J
-vector

\displaystyle T_{3\mu;\nu\alpha}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{1};k_{2},k_{3}\right)+T_{3\mu;\nu\alpha}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{2};k_{3},k_{1}\right)+T_{3\mu;\nu\alpha}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{3};k_{1},k_{2}\right)

(3.254)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(q_{\alpha}p_{\nu}-p_{\alpha}q_{\nu}\right)J_{3\mu}+\left(q_{\nu}p_{\mu}-p_{\nu}q_{\mu}\right)J_{3\alpha}+\left(p_{\alpha}q_{\mu}-q_{\alpha}p_{\mu}\right)J_{3\nu}=0.

As these identities are indeed confirmed, the expectation over the amplitude
also applies

T_{\mu\nu\alpha}^{AVV}=4i\varepsilon_{\mu\alpha XY}T_{3\nu;XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{1};k_{2},k_{3}\right)+4i\varepsilon_{\mu\nu XY}T_{3\alpha;XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{3};k_{1},k_{2}\right)-i\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}T_{\beta}^{VPP}.

(3.255)

If compared with other free indices,
\mu
has a distinct function in this
equation. That is a direct consequence of the trace version adopted in the
integrand exploration (
3.58
)-(
3.57
).

Proceeding to the last substructure, we consult Equation (
3.64
) to
express the
VPP
amplitude as a combination of Feynman integrals

\displaystyle T_{\beta}^{VPP}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2p_{\beta}I_{2\beta}\left(k_{1},k_{2}\right)-4I_{2\beta}\left(k_{1},k_{3}\right)-2\left(k_{1}+k_{3}\right)_{\beta}I_{2}\left(k_{1},k_{3}\right)

(3.256)

\displaystyle+2\left(q-p\right)_{\beta}I_{2}\left(k_{2},k_{3}\right)+2\left(q-p\right)^{2}\left(I_{3\beta}+k_{1\beta}I_{3}\right)

\displaystyle-2q^{2}\left(I_{3\beta}+k_{2\beta}I_{3}\right)+2p^{2}\left(I_{3\beta}+k_{3\beta}I_{3}\right).

Besides results obtained at the outset of the triangle discussion,
two-propagator integrals (
3.225
)-(
3.228
) are also ingredients
needed to build this object. Their replacement leads to the following
mathematical expression:

\displaystyle T_{\beta}^{VPP}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left(k_{1}+k_{3}\right)^{\rho}\Delta_{\beta\rho}-2\left(2p-q\right)_{\beta}I_{\log}

(3.257)

\displaystyle+4\left(p^{2}-p\cdot q\right)J_{3\beta}+2\left(q^{2}p_{\beta}-p^{2}q_{\beta}\right)J_{3}

\displaystyle+i\left(4\pi\right)^{-2}\left[p_{\beta}\xi_{0}^{\left(0\right)}\left(p\right)-\left(q-p\right)_{\beta}\xi_{0}^{\left(0\right)}\left(p-q\right)\right].

Since the dependence on external momenta is not univocal for
\xi_{k}
-functions, we must specify their argument.

As we determined all substructures, renaming indices and organizing
contributions is the final task before expressing the
AVV
amplitude:

\displaystyle T_{\mu\nu\alpha}^{AVV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2i\varepsilon_{\mu\alpha\rho\sigma}\left(p-q\right)^{\rho}\Delta_{\nu}^{\sigma}+2i\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}p^{\rho}\Delta_{\alpha}^{\sigma}

(3.258)

\displaystyle-2i\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\rho}\left(k_{1}+k_{3}\right)_{\sigma}\Delta^{\rho\sigma}-8i\varepsilon_{\mu\alpha\rho\sigma}\left(p-q\right)^{\rho}J_{3\nu}^{\sigma}

\displaystyle+8i\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}p^{\rho}J_{3\alpha}^{\sigma}-8i\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}p^{\rho}q_{\alpha}J_{3\sigma}+8i\varepsilon_{\mu\alpha\rho\sigma}p^{\rho}q^{\sigma}J_{3\nu}

\displaystyle-4i\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\left(p^{2}-p\cdot q\right)J_{3\beta}-2i\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\left(q^{2}p^{\beta}-p^{2}q^{\beta}\right)J_{3}

\displaystyle+2\left(4\pi\right)^{-2}\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\left[p^{\beta}\xi_{0}^{\left(0\right)}\left(p\right)-\left(q-p\right)^{\beta}\xi_{0}^{\left(0\right)}\left(p-q\right)\right].

Some comments on ill-defined quantities are pertinent to conclude this
analysis. Even though it appears when we survey substructures individually,
the irreducible standard object
I_{\log}
does not appear within the final
expression since the corresponding coefficient vanishes. That implies all
divergences concentrate on surface terms
\Delta_{\rho\sigma}
, whose
coefficient unavoidably depends on a non-physical momenta combination.
Interestingly, this ambiguous contribution comes from the vector function
VPP
; standard tensors do not manifest this type of ambiguity.

3.4.5
Four-Point Amplitudes - Feynman Integrals

The final task of this section is to compute quantities typical
of calculations involving four-point amplitudes, starting with the
corresponding Feynman integrals (
3.122
). Most are finite, therefore,
polynomial (
3.164
) manifests after adopting the Feynman parametrization (
3.216
) and grouping terms on the loop momentum

\frac{1}{D_{1234}}=\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dz\int_{0}^{1-z}dy\int_{0}^{1-y-z}dx\text{ }\frac{1}{\left[\left(k+L\right)^{2}+Q\left(x,y,z\right)\right]^{4}},

(3.259)

where
x
,
y
, and
z
are the parameters. The object
L=k_{1}-px-qy-rz

corresponds to the quantity shifted posteriorly to applying the integration.
Notations involving it are nothing more than tools to facilitate the
visualization of mathematical expressions, hence suppressed later when
identifying finite functions. Considering these introductions, explicit
integration leads to the following results:

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dz\int_{0}^{1-z}dy\int_{0}^{1-y-z}dx\text{ }\frac{1}{Q^{2}},

(3.260)

\displaystyle I_{4\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dz\int_{0}^{1-z}dy\int_{0}^{1-y-z}dx\text{ }L_{\mu}\frac{1}{Q^{2}},

(3.261)

\displaystyle I_{4\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dz\int_{0}^{1-z}dy\int_{0}^{1-y-z}dx\left[L_{\mu\nu}\frac{1}{Q^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}\frac{1}{Q}\right],

(3.262)

\displaystyle I_{4\mu\nu\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dz\int_{0}^{1-z}dy\int_{0}^{1-y-z}dx\left[L_{\mu\nu\alpha}\frac{1}{Q^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}L_{\mu\nu\alpha}^{\prime}\frac{1}{Q}\right],

(3.263)

where we compact products involving the momentum
L_{\mu\nu}=L_{\mu}L_{\nu}
and introduce the combination

L_{\mu\nu\alpha}^{\prime}=L_{\mu}g_{\nu\alpha}+L_{\nu}g_{\mu\alpha}+L_{\alpha}g_{\mu\nu}.

(3.264)

We still have to evaluate the 4th-order Feynman integral. Since it exhibits
logarithmic power counting, one form for its separation employs the
N=0

version of identity (
3.129
) to write

\frac{k_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}}{D_{1234}}-\frac{k_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}}{D_{\lambda}^{4}}=-\frac{A_{4}k_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}}{D_{\lambda}^{4}D_{4}}-\frac{A_{3}k_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}}{D_{\lambda}^{3}D_{34}}-\frac{A_{2}k_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}}{D_{\lambda}^{2}D_{234}}-\frac{A_{1}k_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}}{D_{\lambda}D_{1234}}.

(3.265)

As this equation follows the developed strategy, integrating the left-hand
side leads to ill-defined quantities. They receive an organization through
symmetric tensors:

\displaystyle I_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}-\tfrac{1}{24}A_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}-\tfrac{1}{24}g_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}I_{\log}

(3.266)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int\frac{d^{4}k}{\left(2\pi\right)^{4}}\left[\frac{A_{4}k_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}}{D_{\lambda}^{4}D_{4}}+\frac{A_{3}k_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}}{D_{\lambda}^{3}D_{34}}+\frac{A_{2}k_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}}{D_{\lambda}^{2}D_{234}}+\frac{A_{1}k_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}}{D_{\lambda}D_{1234}}\right].

Here, aiming for a cleaner form, we concentrate all surface terms in the
object

A_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}=\square_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}+\tfrac{1}{2}\left(g_{\mu\nu}\Delta_{\alpha\beta}+g_{\mu\alpha}\Delta_{\nu\beta}+g_{\mu\beta}\Delta_{\nu\alpha}g_{\nu\alpha}\Delta_{\mu\beta}+g_{\nu\beta}\Delta_{\mu\alpha}+g_{\alpha\beta}\Delta_{\mu\nu}\right)

(3.267)

while products involving the metric tensor receive a compact notation

g_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}=g_{\mu\nu}g_{\alpha\beta}+g_{\mu\alpha}g_{\nu\beta}+g_{\mu\beta}g_{\nu\alpha}.

(3.268)

Next, proceeding to the finite sector on the right-hand side of this
integral, each rational function requires a different Feynman
parametrization. They differ from the cases above because polynomials depend
on non-physical parameters. This type of contribution cancels out
identically after integrations by parts, which ultimately brings polynomials
dependent on external momenta:

\displaystyle-\int\frac{d^{4}k}{\left(2\pi\right)^{4}}\left[\frac{A_{4}k_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}}{D_{\lambda}^{4}D_{4}}+\frac{A_{3}k_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}}{D_{\lambda}^{3}D_{34}}+\frac{A_{2}k_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}}{D_{\lambda}^{2}D_{234}}+\frac{A_{1}k_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}}{D_{\lambda}D_{1234}}\right]

(3.269)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dz\int_{0}^{1-z}dy\int_{0}^{1-y-z}dx\left[L_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\frac{1}{Q^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}L_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime}\frac{1}{Q}-\frac{1}{4}g_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\ln\frac{Q}{-\lambda^{2}}\right],

where we introduce the object

L_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime}=L_{\mu\nu}g_{\alpha\beta}+L_{\mu\alpha}g_{\nu\beta}+L_{\mu\beta}g_{\nu\alpha}+L_{\nu\alpha}g_{\mu\beta}+L_{\nu\beta}g_{\mu\alpha}+L_{\alpha\beta}g_{\mu\nu}.

(3.270)

That completes the expression for the last Feynman integral

\displaystyle I_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}-\tfrac{1}{24}A_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}-\tfrac{1}{24}g_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}I_{\log}

(3.271)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dz\int_{0}^{1-z}dy\int_{0}^{1-y-z}dx\left[L_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\frac{1}{Q^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}L_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}^{\prime\prime}\frac{1}{Q}-\frac{1}{4}g_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\ln\frac{Q}{-\lambda^{2}}\right].

To complete the systematization of Feynman integrals, let us identify terms
depending exclusively on external momenta and group them into
J
-tensors.
The remaining terms are proportional to combinations of the arbitrary
routing
k_{1}
, connecting to lower-order Feynman integrals. This process
expands the momentum
L
and its combinations, so the notations introduced
above are no longer necessary. Nevertheless, we recur to compact notations
to products involving momenta, e.g.,
k_{1\mu\nu}=k_{1\mu}k_{1\nu}
and
p_{\mu\nu}=p_{\mu}p_{\nu}
. We cast the final forms for the integrals in
the sequence:

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{4},

(3.272)

\displaystyle I_{4\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{4\mu}-k_{1\mu}I_{4},

(3.273)

\displaystyle I_{4\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{4\mu\nu}-\left[k_{1\mu}I_{4\nu}+k_{1\nu}I_{4\mu}\right]-\left[k_{1\mu\nu}I_{4}\right],

(3.274)

\displaystyle I_{4\mu\nu\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{4\mu\nu\alpha}-\left[k_{1\mu}I_{4\nu\alpha}+k_{1\nu}I_{4\alpha\mu}+k_{1\alpha}I_{4\mu\nu}\right]

(3.275)

\displaystyle-\left[k_{1\nu\alpha}I_{4\mu}+k_{1\mu\alpha}I_{4\nu}+k_{1\mu\nu}I_{4\alpha}\right]-\left[k_{1\mu\nu\alpha}I_{4}\right],

\displaystyle I_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}-\tfrac{1}{24}A_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}-\tfrac{1}{24}g_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}I_{\log}

(3.276)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}-\left[k_{1\mu}I_{4\nu\alpha\beta}+k_{1\nu}I_{4\mu\alpha\beta}+k_{1\alpha}I_{4\mu\nu\beta}+k_{1\beta}I_{4\mu\nu\alpha}\right]

\displaystyle-\left[k_{1\nu\alpha\beta}I_{4\mu}+k_{1\mu\alpha\beta}I_{4\nu}+k_{1\mu\nu\beta}I_{4\alpha}+k_{1\mu\nu\alpha}I_{4\beta}\right]-\left[k_{1\mu\nu\alpha\beta}I_{4}\right].

3.4.6

The amplitude

emerges by integrating Equation (

), as

\displaystyle T_{\nu\alpha\beta}^{PVVV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4im\left(g_{\kappa\nu}g_{\alpha\beta}-g_{\kappa\alpha}g_{\nu\beta}+g_{\kappa\beta}g_{\nu\alpha}\right)F_{4\kappa}

\displaystyle+2imF_{4\nu\alpha\beta}-i\varepsilon_{\kappa\nu\alpha\beta}T_{\kappa}^{APPP}.

Its content mirrors the
AVV
triangle since both are 3rd-order

operations performed there find their analogs here.

That is particularly evident for standard tensors with three momenta in the

\displaystyle F_{4\nu\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=

As these coefficients are products between the Levi-Civita symbol and the

\displaystyle F_{4\nu\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4m\varepsilon_{\nu\alpha\beta\rho}\left[\left(r-q\right)^{\rho}\left(J_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}-J_{3}^{\prime\prime}\right)+p^{\rho}\left(J_{3}-J_{3}^{\prime}\right)\right]

(3.301)

\displaystyle-8m\varepsilon_{\alpha\beta\rho\sigma}q^{\rho}r^{\sigma}J_{4\nu}-8m\varepsilon_{\nu\alpha\rho\sigma}p^{\rho}r^{\sigma}J_{4\beta}

\displaystyle+4m\varepsilon_{\nu\alpha\beta\rho}\left[\left(q^{2}-r^{2}-q\cdot r\right)p^{\rho}+\left(p\cdot r-p^{2}\right)q^{\rho}+p^{2}r^{\rho}\right]J_{4}

\displaystyle-4m\varepsilon_{\beta\alpha\rho\sigma}\left(q^{\rho}p_{\nu}-p^{\rho}q_{\nu}\right)r^{\sigma}J_{4}+4m\varepsilon_{\nu\beta\rho\sigma}\left(q^{\rho}p_{\alpha}+p^{\rho}q_{\alpha}\right)r^{\sigma}J_{4}

where we identified

\displaystyle T_{\nu\alpha\beta}^{PVVV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4im\left(g_{\alpha\beta}\varepsilon_{\nu\rho\sigma\kappa}-g_{\nu\beta}\varepsilon_{\alpha\rho\sigma\kappa}+g_{\nu\alpha}\varepsilon_{\beta\rho\sigma\kappa}\right)p^{\rho}q^{\sigma}r^{\kappa}J_{4}

(3.304)

\displaystyle+4im\varepsilon_{\nu\alpha\beta\rho}\left[\left(r-q\right)^{\rho}J_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}+p^{\rho}J_{3}\right]-8im\varepsilon_{\alpha\beta\rho\sigma}q^{\rho}r^{\sigma}J_{4\nu}

\displaystyle-8im\varepsilon_{\nu\alpha\rho\sigma}p^{\rho}r^{\sigma}J_{4\beta}+4im\varepsilon_{\nu\alpha\beta\rho}\left[\left(p\cdot q\right)r^{\rho}-r^{2}p^{\rho}\right]J_{4}

\displaystyle-4im\varepsilon_{\nu\alpha\rho\sigma}\left(q^{\rho}p_{\beta}-p^{\rho}q_{\beta}\right)r^{\sigma}J_{4}.

As anticipated by the analysis of mass dimension, we found a finite

3.4.7
Four-Point Amplitudes -
AVVV

We reach the last correlator that concerns this investigation. From Equation
(
3.101
), we write the integrated version of the
AVVV
amplitude as

\displaystyle T_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}^{AVVV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle iF_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}-i\left[\varepsilon_{\mu\alpha\beta X}T_{X\nu}^{VVPP}+\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\beta X}T_{X\alpha}^{VPVP}+\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha X}T_{X\beta}^{VPPV}\right]

(3.305)

\displaystyle+\left[g_{\alpha\beta}T_{\mu\nu}^{AVPP}+g_{\nu\beta}T_{\mu\alpha}^{APVP}+g_{\nu\alpha}T_{\mu\beta}^{APPV}\right]+2i\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}T^{PPPP}.

Since the involved mathematical expressions are extensive, we focus only on
analyzing substructures without providing the complete object. Although this
presentation follows the same steps from Section (
3.2.6
), we add one
step to discuss 2nd-order tensors before building the corresponding
subamplitudes.

Fourth-Order Standard Tensors

First, we compute all required 4th-order tensors starting with the
simplified version. Besides appearing by itself within
AVPP
-like
functions, this object compounds the standard version required to express
the sector
F_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}
. These are the only places where the
Feynman integral
I_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}
appears; therefore, containing
all contributions symmetric in four indices
10
10

10

The mentioned structures are a combination of surface terms
A_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}
, the irreducible divergent object
I_{\log}
, and the
finite tensor
J_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}
. Consult Equation (
3.266
)
for further information.
. Since most of the involved tensors exhibit
antisymmetry in some indices, we acknowledged the possibility of
cancellation for these contributions. Verifying this prospect is part of our
goal. If this situation indeed occurs, the surface term
\square_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}
and the finite tensor
J_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}
do not
appear in this work.

By expanding products from the numerator of its structure (
3.107
) and
integrating, we recognize the simplified version as a combination of
four-propagator Feynman integrals:

\displaystyle T_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\left(k_{i},k_{j},k_{m},k_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}+\left[k_{i\mu}I_{4\nu\alpha\beta}+k_{j\nu}I_{4\mu\alpha\beta}+k_{m\alpha}I_{4\mu\nu\beta}+k_{n\beta}I_{4\mu\nu\alpha}\right]

(3.306)

\displaystyle+\left[k_{i\mu}k_{j\nu}I_{4\alpha\beta}+k_{i\mu}k_{m\alpha}I_{4\nu\beta}+k_{i\mu}k_{n\beta}I_{4\nu\alpha}+k_{j\nu}k_{m\alpha}I_{4\mu\beta}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+k_{j\nu}k_{n\beta}I_{4\mu\alpha}+k_{m\alpha}k_{n\beta}I_{4\mu\nu}\right]+\left[k_{j\nu}k_{m\alpha}k_{n\beta}I_{4\mu}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+k_{i\mu}k_{m\alpha}k_{n\beta}I_{4\nu}+k_{i\mu}k_{j\nu}k_{n\beta}I_{4\alpha}+k_{i\mu}k_{j\nu}k_{m\alpha}I_{4\beta}\right]

\displaystyle+k_{i\mu}k_{j\nu}k_{m\alpha}k_{n\beta}I_{4}.

Next, our task consists of substituting their explicit expressions (
3.272
)-(
3.276
) while obeying the hierarchy observed in previous cases;
consult Equation (
3.242
). This strategy allows writing all finite
structures through
J
-tensors with external momenta
p_{i}=k_{1}-k_{i}
as
coefficients. Observe that the
J
-scalar does not contribute due to the
unavoidable dependence on
p_{1}=0
. Once these ideas are clear, we
introduce the simplified version

\displaystyle T_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\left(k_{i},k_{j},k_{m},k_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{24}A_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}+\tfrac{1}{24}g_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}I_{\log}+J_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}

(3.307)

\displaystyle-\left[p_{i\mu}J_{4\nu\alpha\beta}+p_{j\nu}J_{4\mu\alpha\beta}+p_{m\alpha}J_{4\mu\nu\beta}+p_{n\beta}J_{4\mu\nu\alpha}\right]

\displaystyle+\left[p_{i\mu}p_{j\nu}J_{4\alpha\beta}+p_{i\mu}p_{m\alpha}J_{4\nu\beta}+p_{i\mu}p_{n\beta}J_{4\nu\alpha}+p_{j\nu}p_{m\alpha}J_{4\mu\beta}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+p_{j\nu}p_{n\beta}J_{4\mu\alpha}+p_{m\alpha}p_{n\beta}J_{4\mu\nu}\right]-\left[p_{j\nu}p_{m\alpha}p_{n\beta}J_{4\mu}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+p_{i\mu}p_{m\alpha}p_{n\beta}J_{4\nu}+p_{i\mu}p_{j\nu}p_{n\beta}J_{4\alpha}+p_{i\mu}p_{j\nu}p_{m\alpha}J_{4\beta}\right],

and all necessary momenta configurations

\displaystyle T_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\left(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{24}A_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}+\tfrac{1}{24}g_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}I_{\log}+J_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}

(3.308)

\displaystyle-\left[p_{\nu}J_{4\mu\alpha\beta}+q_{\alpha}J_{4\mu\nu\beta}+r_{\beta}J_{4\mu\nu\alpha}\right]

\displaystyle+\left[p_{\nu}q_{\alpha}J_{4\mu\beta}+p_{\nu}r_{\beta}J_{4\mu\alpha}+q_{\alpha}r_{\beta}J_{4\mu\nu}\right]-p_{\nu}q_{\alpha}r_{\beta}J_{4\mu},

\displaystyle T_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\left(k_{1},k_{2},k_{4},k_{3}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{24}A_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}+\tfrac{1}{24}g_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}I_{\log}+J_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}

(3.309)

\displaystyle-\left[p_{\nu}J_{4\mu\alpha\beta}+r_{\alpha}J_{4\mu\nu\beta}+q_{\beta}J_{4\mu\nu\alpha}\right]

\displaystyle+\left[p_{\nu}r_{\alpha}J_{4\mu\beta}+p_{\nu}q_{\beta}J_{4\mu\alpha}+r_{\alpha}q_{\beta}J_{4\mu\nu}\right]-p_{\nu}r_{\alpha}q_{\beta}J_{4\mu},

\displaystyle T_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\left(k_{2},k_{1},k_{3},k_{4}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{24}A_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}+\tfrac{1}{24}g_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}I_{\log}+J_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}

(3.310)

\displaystyle-\left[p_{\mu}J_{4\nu\alpha\beta}+q_{\alpha}J_{4\mu\nu\beta}+r_{\beta}J_{4\mu\nu\alpha}\right]

\displaystyle+\left[p_{\mu}q_{\alpha}J_{4\nu\beta}+p_{\mu}r_{\beta}J_{4\nu\alpha}+q_{\alpha}r_{\beta}J_{4\mu\nu}\right]-p_{\mu}q_{\alpha}r_{\beta}J_{4\nu},

\displaystyle T_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\left(k_{2},k_{1},k_{4},k_{3}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{24}A_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}+\tfrac{1}{24}g_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}I_{\log}+J_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}

(3.311)

\displaystyle-\left[p_{\mu}J_{4\nu\alpha\beta}+r_{\alpha}J_{4\mu\nu\beta}+q_{\beta}J_{4\mu\nu\alpha}\right]

\displaystyle+\left[p_{\mu}r_{\alpha}J_{4\nu\beta}+p_{\mu}q_{\beta}J_{4\nu\alpha}+r_{\alpha}q_{\beta}J_{4\mu\nu}\right]-p_{\mu}r_{\alpha}q_{\beta}J_{4\nu}.

Contributions symmetric in four indices come from the highest-order
integral, appearing in the first row from the equations above. We stress
that version (
3.308
) appears contracted to the Levi-Civita symbol with
AVPP
-type amplitudes; see Equation (
3.115
). That implies symmetric
contributions vanish, but we return to this discussion in due time.

With these tools determined, let us obtain the standard version that admits
sign choices (
3.106
). By integrating Equation (
3.108
), we write
this object through the following combination:

\displaystyle T_{4\mu\nu;\alpha\beta}^{\left(s_{1},s_{2}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\left(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\right)+s_{1}T_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\left(k_{2},k_{1},k_{3},k_{4}\right)

(3.312)

\displaystyle+s_{2}T_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\left(k_{1},k_{2},k_{4},k_{3}\right)+s_{1}s_{2}T_{4\mu\nu;\alpha\beta}\left(k_{2},k_{1},k_{4},k_{3}\right).

We omit arguments exhibiting the momenta hierarchy
T_{4\mu\nu;\alpha\beta}^{\left(s_{1};s_{2}\right)}=T_{4\mu\nu;\alpha\beta}^{\left(s_{1};s_{2}\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{2};k_{3},k_{4}\right)
. Then, our job
consists of replacing expressions attributed to different momenta
configurations. This operation produces the generic form

\displaystyle T_{4\mu\nu;\alpha\beta}^{\left(s_{1},s_{2}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1+s_{1}\right)\left(1+s_{2}\right)\left[\tfrac{1}{24}A_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}+\tfrac{1}{24}g_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}I_{\log}+J_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\right]

(3.313)

\displaystyle-\left(1+s_{2}\right)\left[s_{1}p_{\mu}J_{4\nu\alpha\beta}+p_{\nu}J_{4\mu\alpha\beta}\right]

\displaystyle-\left(1+s_{1}\right)\left[\left(q_{\alpha}+s_{2}r_{\alpha}\right)J_{4\mu\nu\beta}+\left(r_{\beta}+s_{2}q_{\beta}\right)J_{4\mu\nu\alpha}\right]

\displaystyle+\left(1+s_{1}\right)\left(q_{\alpha}r_{\beta}+s_{2}q_{\beta}r_{\alpha}\right)J_{4\mu\nu}+\left(r_{\beta}+s_{2}q_{\beta}\right)\left(p_{\nu}J_{4\mu\alpha}+s_{1}p_{\mu}J_{4\nu\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(q_{\alpha}+s_{2}r_{\alpha}\right)\left(p_{\nu}J_{4\mu\beta}+s_{1}p_{\mu}J_{4\nu\beta}\right)

\displaystyle-\left(q_{\alpha}r_{\beta}+s_{2}r_{\alpha}q_{\beta}\right)\left(p_{\nu}J_{4\mu}+s_{1}p_{\mu}J_{4\nu}\right);

hence, setting the signs leads to four particular forms

\displaystyle T_{4\mu\nu;\alpha\beta}^{\left(+,+\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{6}A_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}+\tfrac{1}{6}g_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}I_{\log}+4J_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}

(3.314)

\displaystyle-2\left[p_{\mu}J_{4\nu\alpha\beta}+p_{\nu}J_{4\mu\alpha\beta}\right]-2\left[\left(q_{\alpha}+r_{\alpha}\right)J_{4\mu\nu\beta}+\left(r_{\beta}+q_{\beta}\right)J_{4\mu\nu\alpha}\right]

\displaystyle+2\left(q_{\alpha}r_{\beta}+q_{\beta}r_{\alpha}\right)J_{4\mu\nu}+\left(r_{\beta}+q_{\beta}\right)\left(p_{\nu}J_{4\mu\alpha}+p_{\mu}J_{4\nu\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(q_{\alpha}+r_{\alpha}\right)\left(p_{\nu}J_{4\mu\beta}+p_{\mu}J_{4\nu\beta}\right)-\left(q_{\alpha}r_{\beta}+r_{\alpha}q_{\beta}\right)\left(p_{\nu}J_{4\mu}+p_{\mu}J_{4\nu}\right),

\displaystyle T_{4\mu\nu;\alpha\beta}^{\left(+,-\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\left[\left(q_{\alpha}-r_{\alpha}\right)J_{4\mu\nu\beta}+\left(r_{\beta}-q_{\beta}\right)J_{4\mu\nu\alpha}\right]+2\left(q_{\alpha}r_{\beta}-q_{\beta}r_{\alpha}\right)J_{4\mu\nu}

(3.315)

\displaystyle+\left(r_{\beta}-q_{\beta}\right)\left(p_{\nu}J_{4\mu\alpha}+p_{\mu}J_{4\nu\alpha}\right)+\left(q_{\alpha}-r_{\alpha}\right)\left(p_{\nu}J_{4\mu\beta}+p_{\mu}J_{4\nu\beta}\right)

\displaystyle-\left(q_{\alpha}r_{\beta}-r_{\alpha}q_{\beta}\right)\left(p_{\nu}J_{4\mu}+p_{\mu}J_{4\nu}\right),

\displaystyle T_{4\mu\nu;\alpha\beta}^{\left(-,+\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left(p_{\mu}J_{4\nu\alpha\beta}-p_{\nu}J_{4\mu\alpha\beta}\right)+\left(r_{\beta}+q_{\beta}\right)\left(p_{\nu}J_{4\mu\alpha}-p_{\mu}J_{4\nu\alpha}\right)

(3.316)

\displaystyle+\left(q_{\alpha}+r_{\alpha}\right)\left(p_{\nu}J_{4\mu\beta}-p_{\mu}J_{4\nu\beta}\right)-\left(q_{\alpha}r_{\beta}+r_{\alpha}q_{\beta}\right)\left(p_{\nu}J_{4\mu}-p_{\mu}J_{4\nu}\right),

\displaystyle T_{4\mu\nu;\alpha\beta}^{\left(-,-\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(r_{\beta}-q_{\beta}\right)\left(p_{\nu}J_{4\mu\alpha}-p_{\mu}J_{4\nu\alpha}\right)+\left(q_{\alpha}-r_{\alpha}\right)\left(p_{\nu}J_{4\mu\beta}-p_{\mu}J_{4\nu\beta}\right)

(3.317)

\displaystyle-\left(q_{\alpha}r_{\beta}-r_{\alpha}q_{\beta}\right)\left(p_{\nu}J_{4\mu}-p_{\mu}J_{4\nu}\right).

Lastly, from Equations (
3.102
)-(
3.105
), we aim to determine the
entire sector

F_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}=4\varepsilon_{\mu\nu XY}T_{XY\alpha\beta}^{\left(12\right)}+4\varepsilon_{\mu\alpha XY}T_{XY\nu\beta}^{\left(13\right)}+4\varepsilon_{\mu\beta XY}T_{XY\nu\alpha}^{\left(14\right)}.

(3.318)

Each of its pieces relates to a combination of standard tensors
T_{4\mu\nu;\alpha\beta}^{\left(s_{1},s_{2}\right)}=T_{4\mu\nu;\alpha\beta}^{\left(s_{1},s_{2}\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{2};k_{3},k_{4}\right)
:

\displaystyle 2T_{XY\alpha\beta}^{\left(12\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{4XY;\alpha\beta}^{\left(-;+\right)}+T_{4X\alpha;Y\beta}^{\left(-;+\right)}-T_{4X\beta;Y\alpha}^{\left(-;-\right)}+T_{4\alpha Y;\beta X}^{\left(-;+\right)}+T_{4\beta Y;\alpha X}^{\left(-;-\right)}+T_{4\alpha\beta;XY}^{\left(-;-\right)},

(3.319)

\displaystyle 2T_{XY\nu\beta}^{\left(13\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{4XY;\nu\beta}^{\left(-;+\right)}+T_{4Y\beta;\nu X}^{\left(-;-\right)}-T_{4\nu Y;\beta X}^{\left(+;+\right)}-T_{4\beta X;Y\nu}^{\left(-;-\right)}+T_{4\nu X;Y\beta}^{\left(+;+\right)}-T_{4\nu\beta;XY}^{\left(+;-\right)},

(3.320)

\displaystyle 2T_{XY\nu\alpha}^{\left(14\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{4XY;\nu\alpha}^{\left(-;-\right)}+T_{4\alpha Y;\nu X}^{\left(-;-\right)}-T_{4\nu Y;\alpha X}^{\left(+;-\right)}+T_{4\alpha X;Y\nu}^{\left(-;-\right)}-T_{4\nu X;Y\alpha}^{\left(+;-\right)}+T_{4\nu\alpha;XY}^{\left(+;-\right)}.

(3.321)

We highlight that the tensor with
s_{1}=s_{2}=+1
is the only one
containing structures symmetric in four indices; thus, it is straightforward
to verify their cancellation within object
T_{XY\nu\beta}^{\left(13\right)}
. The immediate consequence is that the entire sector consists
of a finite object. Considering our comment on
AVPP
-like amplitudes, this
result completes the proof that the surface term
\square_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}
and the finite tensor
J_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}
do not appear in
this work.

Since all tensors exhibit the same momenta configuration, no additional
ingredients are necessary for their evaluation. We only have to rename
indices of the particular versions of the standard tensor (with signs set)
and perform the replacements. As the adopted notations emphasize contracted
indices through uppercase Latin letters, simplifications associated with
symmetry properties are evident. After performing them, we present the final
expressions attributed to the tensors below. Arrows indicate that only
non-trivial contributions regarding contractions appear, which is compatible
with Equation (
3.318
).

\displaystyle T_{XY\alpha\beta}^{\left(12\right)}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle 4p_{X}J_{4Y\alpha\beta}-2\left(p_{\alpha}q_{X}+q_{\alpha}p_{X}\right)J_{4Y\beta}

(3.322)

\displaystyle-2\left[\left(q+r\right)_{\beta}p_{X}-p_{\beta}\left(q-r\right)_{X}\right]J_{4Y\alpha}+2r_{X}p_{Y}J_{4\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle+\left[\left(q_{\alpha}r_{\beta}+r_{\alpha}q_{\beta}\right)p_{X}+\left(r_{\beta}p_{\alpha}-r_{\alpha}p_{\beta}\right)q_{X}+\left(q_{\beta}p_{\alpha}+q_{\alpha}p_{\beta}\right)r_{X}\right]J_{4Y}

\displaystyle+\left(q_{\beta}p_{X}r_{Y}+r_{\beta}p_{X}q_{Y}+p_{\beta}r_{X}q_{Y}\right)J_{4\alpha}

\displaystyle+\left(q_{\alpha}p_{X}r_{Y}+r_{\alpha}q_{X}p_{Y}+p_{\alpha}q_{X}r_{Y}\right)J_{4\beta}

\displaystyle T_{XY\nu\beta}^{\left(13\right)}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle 4\left(q-p\right)_{X}J_{4Y\nu\beta}+2\left(q_{\nu}p_{X}-p_{\nu}q_{X}\right)J_{4Y\beta}

(3.323)

\displaystyle+2\left[\left(q+r\right)_{\beta}p_{X}-\left(p+r\right)_{\beta}q_{X}+\left(p-q\right)_{\beta}r_{X}\right]J_{4Y\nu}+2\left(p-q\right)_{X}r_{Y}J_{4\nu\beta}

\displaystyle-\left[\left(q_{\nu}r_{\beta}+r_{\nu}q_{\beta}\right)p_{X}-\left(r_{\beta}p_{\nu}+r_{\nu}p_{\beta}\right)q_{X}+\left(q_{\nu}p_{\beta}-q_{\beta}p_{\nu}\right)r_{X}\right]J_{4Y}

\displaystyle+\left(q_{\beta}r_{X}p_{Y}+r_{\beta}q_{X}p_{Y}+q_{X}r_{Y}p_{\beta}\right)J_{4\nu}

\displaystyle+\left(p_{\nu}q_{X}r_{Y}+q_{\nu}r_{X}p_{Y}+r_{\nu}p_{X}q_{Y}\right)J_{4\beta}

\displaystyle T_{XY\nu\alpha}^{\left(14\right)}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle 4\left(r-q\right)_{X}J_{4Y\nu\alpha}-2\left[\left(q-r\right)_{\nu}p_{X}-p_{\nu}\left(q-r\right)_{X}\right]J_{4Y\alpha}

(3.324)

\displaystyle+2\left[\left(q-r\right)_{\alpha}p_{X}+\left(p+r\right)_{\alpha}q_{X}-\left(p+q\right)_{\alpha}r_{X}\right]J_{4Y\nu}+2q_{X}r_{Y}J_{4\nu\alpha}

\displaystyle+\left[\left(q_{\nu}r_{\alpha}-r_{\nu}q_{\alpha}\right)p_{X}-\left(r_{\nu}p_{\alpha}+r_{\alpha}p_{\nu}\right)q_{X}+\left(q_{\nu}p_{\alpha}+q_{\alpha}p_{\nu}\right)r_{X}\right]J_{4Y}

\displaystyle+\left(q_{\alpha}r_{X}p_{Y}+r_{\alpha}p_{X}q_{Y}+p_{\alpha}r_{X}q_{Y}\right)J_{4\nu}

\displaystyle+\left(q_{\nu}p_{X}r_{Y}+r_{\nu}q_{X}p_{Y}+p_{\nu}r_{X}q_{Y}\right)J_{4\alpha}

Second-Order Standard Tensors

Second, we compute the 2nd-order standard tensors required to
build up subamplitudes. Even though we already examined those involving
three propagators, we get back to this subject as the perspective is broader
this time. For this purpose, recall the general form obtained succeeding the
integration (
3.242
)

\displaystyle T_{3\mu\nu}^{\left(s\right)}\left(k_{i},k_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\left(1+s\right)\left(\Delta_{\mu\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}I_{\log}+4J_{3\mu\nu}\right)

(3.325)

\displaystyle-\left(p_{j}+sp_{i}\right)_{\nu}J_{3\mu}-\left(p_{i}+sp_{j}\right)_{\mu}J_{3\nu}+\left(p_{i\mu}p_{j\nu}+sp_{i\nu}p_{j\mu}\right)J_{3},

where associations with external momenta occur through the relation
p_{i}=k_{1}-k_{i}
.

We assigned a special role for the routing
k_{1}
simply because it is the
first to appear in the adopted ordering. This reasoning was implicit when
evaluating three-point Feynman integrals in Subsection (
3.4.2
) and led
to the external momenta
p
and
q
. The notation for the corresponding
functions is
\xi_{ab}=\xi_{ab}\left(p,q\right)
and reflects in the
corresponding
J_{3}
-tensors, including the coefficients inside them.

From the first case, let us obtain the second
D_{124}
through the
transformation
k_{3}\rightarrow k_{4}
. That changes the second external
momentum
q\rightarrow r
, which reflects on the notations for functions
\xi_{ab}^{\prime}=\xi_{ab}\left(p,r\right)
and
J_{3}^{\prime}
-tensors. Analogously, the third case
D_{134}
links to the momenta
q
and

r
seen in functions
\xi_{ab}^{\prime\prime}=\xi_{ab}\left(q,r\right)
and
J_{3}^{\prime\prime}
-tensors.

Nevertheless, things are different for objects involving the fourth
denominator
D_{234}
. When emphasizing the routing
k_{2}
, these
particular associations come with
p_{i}^{\prime}=k_{2}-k_{i}=p_{i}-p
and
lead to momenta
q-p
and
r-p
. The notation for functions
\xi_{ab}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\xi_{ab}\left(q-p,r-p\right)
and
J_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}
-tensors follows previous cases; however, the
differences
p_{i}^{\prime}
generate more structures inside the tensors.
We must consider such information when exploring reductions and other
algebraic manipulations.

The generality brought by
J
-tensors makes extensions of the expression
above direct. Besides changing the versions of these tensors, we recall that
there are no ill-defined contributions for the standard tensor depending on
four propagators. Therefore, the new version is the following

T_{4\mu\nu}^{\left(s\right)}\left(k_{i},k_{j}\right)=\left(1+s\right)J_{4\mu\nu}-\left(p_{j}+sp_{i}\right)_{\nu}J_{4\mu}-\left(p_{i}+sp_{j}\right)_{\mu}J_{4\nu}+\left(p_{i\mu}p_{j\nu}+sp_{i\nu}p_{j\mu}\right)J_{4},

(3.326)

where the original association
p_{i}=k_{1}-k_{i}
applies.

Without setting signs, we cast all available momenta configurations for
these objects in the sequence. The line notation (
3.171
) is
particularly advantageous in this scene.

\bullet
Three propagators
D_{123}
-
\xi_{ab}=\xi_{ab}\left(p,q\right)

\displaystyle\left[T_{3\mu\nu}^{\left(s\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\left(1+s\right)\left(\Delta_{\mu\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}I_{\log}+4J_{3\mu\nu}\right)-p_{\nu}J_{3\mu}-sp_{\mu}J_{3\nu}

(3.327)

\displaystyle\left[T_{3\mu\nu}^{\left(s\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{3}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\left(1+s\right)\left(\Delta_{\mu\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}I_{\log}+4J_{3\mu\nu}\right)-q_{\nu}J_{3\mu}-sq_{\mu}J_{3\nu}

(3.328)

\displaystyle\left[T_{3\mu\nu}^{\left(s\right)}\left(k_{2},k_{3}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\left(1+s\right)\left(\Delta_{\mu\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}I_{\log}+4J_{3\mu\nu}\right)

(3.329)

\displaystyle-\left(q+sp\right)_{\nu}J_{3\mu}-\left(p+sq\right)_{\mu}J_{3\nu}+\left(p_{\mu}q_{\nu}+sp_{\nu}q_{\mu}\right)J_{3}

\bullet
Three propagators
D_{124}
-
\xi_{ab}^{\prime}=\xi_{ab}\left(p,r\right)

\displaystyle\left[T_{3\mu\nu}^{\left(s\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{2}\right)\right]^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\left(1+s\right)\left(\Delta_{\mu\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}I_{\log}+4J_{3\mu\nu}^{\prime}\right)-p_{\nu}J_{3\mu}^{\prime}-sp_{\mu}J_{3\nu}^{\prime}

(3.330)

\displaystyle\left[T_{3\mu\nu}^{\left(s\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{4}\right)\right]^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\left(1+s\right)\left(\Delta_{\mu\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}I_{\log}+4J_{3\mu\nu}^{\prime}\right)-r_{\nu}J_{3\mu}^{\prime}-sr_{\mu}J_{3\nu}^{\prime}

(3.331)

\displaystyle\left[T_{3\mu\nu}^{\left(s\right)}\left(k_{2},k_{4}\right)\right]^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\left(1+s\right)\left(\Delta_{\mu\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}I_{\log}+4J_{3\mu\nu}^{\prime}\right)

(3.332)

\displaystyle-\left(r+sp\right)_{\nu}J_{3\mu}^{\prime}-\left(p+sr\right)_{\mu}J_{3\nu}^{\prime}+\left(p_{\mu}r_{\nu}+sp_{\nu}r_{\mu}\right)J_{3}^{\prime}

\bullet
Three propagators
D_{134}
-
\xi_{ab}^{\prime\prime}=\xi_{ab}\left(q,r\right)

\displaystyle\left[T_{3\mu\nu}^{\left(s\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{3}\right)\right]^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\left(1+s\right)\left(\Delta_{\mu\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}I_{\log}+4J_{3\mu\nu}^{\prime\prime}\right)-q_{\nu}J_{3\mu}^{\prime\prime}-sq_{\mu}J_{3\nu}^{\prime\prime}

(3.333)

\displaystyle\left[T_{3\mu\nu}^{\left(s\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{4}\right)\right]^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\left(1+s\right)\left(\Delta_{\mu\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}I_{\log}+4J_{3\mu\nu}^{\prime\prime}\right)-r_{\nu}J_{3\mu}^{\prime\prime}-sr_{\mu}J_{3\nu}^{\prime\prime}

(3.334)

\displaystyle\left[T_{3\mu\nu}^{\left(s\right)}\left(k_{3},k_{4}\right)\right]^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\left(1+s\right)\left(\Delta_{\mu\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}I_{\log}+4J_{3\mu\nu}^{\prime\prime}\right)

(3.335)

\displaystyle-\left(r+sq\right)_{\nu}J_{3\mu}^{\prime\prime}-\left(q+sr\right)_{\mu}J_{3\nu}^{\prime\prime}+\left(q_{\mu}r_{\nu}+sq_{\nu}r_{\mu}\right)J_{3}^{\prime\prime}

\bullet
Three propagators
D_{234}
-
\xi_{ab}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\xi_{ab}\left(q-p,r-p\right)

\displaystyle\left[T_{3\mu\nu}^{\left(s\right)}\left(k_{2},k_{3}\right)\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\left(1+s\right)\left(\Delta_{\mu\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}I_{\log}+4J_{3\mu\nu}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)

(3.336)

\displaystyle-\left(q-p\right)_{\nu}J_{3\mu}^{\prime\prime\prime}-s\left(q-p\right)_{\mu}J_{3\nu}^{\prime\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\left[T_{3\mu\nu}^{\left(s\right)}\left(k_{2},k_{4}\right)\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\left(1+s\right)\left(\Delta_{\mu\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}I_{\log}+4J_{3\mu\nu}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)

(3.337)

\displaystyle-\left(r-p\right)_{\nu}J_{3\mu}^{\prime\prime\prime}-s\left(r-p\right)_{\mu}J_{3\nu}^{\prime\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\left[T_{3\mu\nu}^{\left(s\right)}\left(k_{3},k_{4}\right)\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\left(1+s\right)\left(\Delta_{\mu\nu}+g_{\mu\nu}I_{\log}+4J_{3\mu\nu}^{\prime\prime\prime}\right)

(3.338)

\displaystyle-\left[\left(r-p\right)+s\left(q-p\right)\right]_{\nu}J_{3\mu}^{\prime\prime\prime}-\left[\left(q-p\right)+s\left(r-p\right)\right]_{\mu}J_{3\nu}^{\prime\prime\prime}

\displaystyle+\left[\left(1+s\right)p_{\mu}p_{\nu}-\left(p_{\nu}q_{\mu}+sp_{\mu}q_{\nu}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\left(p_{\mu}r_{\nu}+sp_{\nu}r_{\mu}\right)+\left(q_{\mu}r_{\nu}+sq_{\nu}r_{\mu}\right)\right]J_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}

\bullet

-
\xi_{abc}=\xi_{abc}\left(p,q,r\right)

\displaystyle\left(1+s\right)J_{4\mu\nu}-p_{\nu}J_{4\mu}-sp_{\mu}J_{4\nu}

(3.339)

\displaystyle\left(1+s\right)J_{4\mu\nu}-\left(r+sp\right)_{\nu}J_{4\mu}

Odd Amplitudes -

,

, and

Forth, we compute odd amplitudes that are 2nd-order tensors:
AVPP
,
APVP
, and
APPV
. Given the general form (
3.114
), the
integral operation characterizes two sectors corresponding to different
tensor structures:

T_{\mu\nu}^{\Gamma_{i}\Gamma_{j}\Gamma_{k}\Gamma_{l}}=is_{1}\varepsilon_{\mu XYZ}F_{4\nu XYZ}^{\left(s_{2},s_{3}\right)}+is_{1}\varepsilon_{\mu\nu XY}F_{4XY}.

(3.350)

We distinguish particular functions when choosing signs through the
association: the
AVPP
function by fixing
s_{i}=\left(-1,-1,+1\right)
,
the
APVP
by fixing
s_{i}=\left(+1,+1,+1\right)
, and the
APPV
by
fixing
s_{i}=\left(-1,+1,-1\right)
.

The first sector is proportional to the simplified version of the 4th-order
standard tensor (
3.308
):

\varepsilon_{\mu XYZ}F_{4\nu XYZ}^{\left(s_{2},s_{3}\right)}=4\left(-\varepsilon_{\mu BCD}g_{\nu A}+s_{2}\varepsilon_{\mu ACD}g_{\nu B}+s_{3}\varepsilon_{\mu ABD}g_{\nu C}-\varepsilon_{\mu ABC}g_{\nu D}\right)T_{4ABCD}.

(3.351)

Following its replacement, symmetry properties bring simplifications so this
product assumes the general form

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mu XYZ}F_{4\nu XYZ}^{\left(s_{2},s_{3}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4\varepsilon_{\mu XYZ}\left[\left(1-s_{2}\right)q_{Y}r_{Z}J_{4\nu X}+\left(1+s_{3}\right)p_{X}r_{Z}J_{4\nu Y}\right.

(3.352)

\displaystyle+2p_{X}q_{Y}J_{4\nu Z}-p_{X}q_{Y}r_{Z}J_{4\nu}+s_{2}p_{\nu}q_{Y}r_{Z}J_{4X}

\displaystyle\left.-s_{3}q_{\nu}p_{X}r_{Z}J_{4Y}-r_{\nu}p_{X}q_{Y}J_{4Z}\right].

As mentioned before, symmetric objects
J_{4\mu\nu\alpha\beta}
and
\square_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}
disappear and do not concern this
investigation. Moving on to the second sector, we have another combination
of 2nd-order standard tensors

\displaystyle F_{4XY}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[T_{3XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{2},k_{3}\right)-T_{3XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{3}\right)+T_{3XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{2}\right)\right]

(3.353)

\displaystyle+\left[-T_{3XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{2},k_{4}\right)+T_{3XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{4}\right)+T_{3XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{2}\right)\right]^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\left[T_{3XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{3},k_{4}\right)+T_{3XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{4}\right)-T_{3XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{3}\right)\right]^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle+\left[T_{3XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{3},k_{4}\right)-T_{3XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{2},k_{4}\right)+T_{3XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{2},k_{3}\right)\right]^{\prime\prime\prime}

\displaystyle+\left[-p^{2}T_{4XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{3},k_{4}\right)+q^{2}T_{4XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{2},k_{4}\right)-r^{2}T_{4XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{2},k_{3}\right)\right.

\displaystyle-\left(p-q\right)^{2}T_{4XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{4}\right)+\left(p-r\right)^{2}T_{4XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{3}\right)

\displaystyle\left.-\left(q-r\right)^{2}T_{4XY}^{\left(-\right)}\left(k_{1},k_{2}\right)\right].

Its structure arises after replacing results from Subsubsection (
3.4.7
) and performing simplifications:

\displaystyle F_{4XY}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4p_{X}J_{3Y}^{\prime}+4\left(r-q\right)_{X}J_{3Y}^{\prime\prime}+2p_{X}q_{Y}J_{3}+2r_{X}p_{Y}J_{3}^{\prime}

(3.354)

\displaystyle+2q_{X}r_{Y}J_{3}^{\prime\prime}+2\left(q_{X}r_{Y}+r_{X}p_{Y}+p_{X}q_{Y}\right)J_{3}^{\prime\prime\prime}

\displaystyle-4\left[\left(q^{2}-q\cdot r\right)p_{X}-\left(p^{2}-p\cdot r\right)q_{X}+\left(p^{2}-p\cdot q\right)r_{X}\right]J_{4Y}

\displaystyle-2\left(p^{2}q_{X}r_{Y}+q^{2}r_{X}p_{Y}+r^{2}p_{X}q_{Y}\right)J_{4}.

Adjusting signs, we cast the final expressions attributed to odd
perturbative amplitudes below.

\bullet
The
AVPP
Amplitude

\displaystyle T_{\mu\nu}^{AVPP}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4i\varepsilon_{\mu XYZ}\left(2q_{Y}r_{Z}J_{4\nu X}+2p_{X}r_{Z}J_{4\nu Y}+2p_{X}q_{Y}J_{4\nu Z}-p_{X}q_{Y}r_{Z}J_{4\nu}\right)

(3.355)

\displaystyle+4i\varepsilon_{\mu XYZ}\left(-p_{\nu}q_{Y}r_{Z}J_{4X}-q_{\nu}p_{X}r_{Z}J_{4Y}-r_{\nu}p_{X}q_{Y}J_{4Z}\right)

APVP

\displaystyle T_{\mu\alpha}^{APVP}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4i\varepsilon_{\mu XYZ}\left(2p_{X}r_{Z}J_{4\alpha Y}+2p_{X}q_{Y}J_{4\alpha Z}-p_{X}q_{Y}r_{Z}J_{4\alpha}\right)

(3.356)

\displaystyle-4i\varepsilon_{\mu XYZ}\left(p_{\alpha}q_{Y}r_{Z}J_{4X}-q_{\alpha}p_{X}r_{Z}J_{4Y}-r_{\alpha}p_{X}q_{Y}J_{4Z}\right)
